<div class='question question'>

Mr Green goes into a café and buys one pack of sandwiches, one 
biscuit and two cups of tea and is charged $\pounds 5.80$.

Mrs Plum goes into the café and buys two packs of sandwiches, two 
biscuits and one cup of tea and is charged $\pounds 6.50$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much would it cost for one pack of sandwiches, one biscuit and one cup of tea?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of a pack of sandwiches $= s$

Let the cost of a biscuit $= b$

Let the cost of a cup of tea $= t$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t  &= \pounds 5.80 \\\\
2s + 2b + t &= \pounds 6.50
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t + 2s + 2b + t    &= 5.80 + 6.50 \\\\
3s + 3b + 3t                &= 12.30 \\\\
3 \times (s + b + t)        &= 12.30 \\\\
s + b + t                   &= \dfrac {12.30} {3} \\\\
s + b + t                   &= \pounds 4.10
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of $1$ pack of sandwiches, $1$ biscuit and $1$ cup of tea $= \pounds 4.10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of a pack of sandwiches $= s$
<emptyline>
Let the cost of a biscuit $= b$
<emptyline>
Let the cost of a cup of tea $= t$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t  &= \pounds 5.80 \\\\
2s + 2b + t &= \pounds 6.50
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t + 2s + 2b + t    &= 5.80 + 6.50 \\\\
3s + 3b + 3t                &= 12.30 \\\\
3 \times (s + b + t)        &= 12.30 \\\\
s + b + t                   &= \dfrac {12.30} {3} \\\\
s + b + t                   &= \pounds 4.10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of $1$ pack of sandwiches, $1$ biscuit and $1$ cup of tea $= \pounds 4.10$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 4.10$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much is a pack of sandwiches and a biscuit?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the above answer,

$
\begin{aligned}
2s + 2b + t  &= \pounds 6.50 \\\\
s + b + t    &= \pounds 4.10 
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
2s + 2b + t - s - b - t     &= 6.50 - 4.10 \\\\
s + b                       &= \pounds 2.40
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of $1$ pack of sandwiches and $1$ biscuit $= \pounds 2.40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2.40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the above answer,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2s + 2b + t  &= \pounds 6.50 \\\\
s + b + t    &= \pounds 4.10 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2s + 2b + t - s - b - t     &= 6.50 - 4.10 \\\\
s + b                       &= \pounds 2.40
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of $1$ pack of sandwiches and $1$ biscuit $= \pounds 2.40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 2.40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much is a cup of tea?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the above answers,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t  &= \pounds 5.80 \\\\
s + b + t   &= \pounds 4.10
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t - s - b - t  &= 5.80 - 4.10 \\\\
t                       &= \pounds 1.70
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of a cup of tea $= \pounds 1.70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the above answers,

$
<emptyline>
We have got $3$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve. Let's reduce it to just $1$ equation,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
s + b + 2t - s - b - t  &= 5.80 - 4.10 \\\\
t                       &= \pounds 1.70
\end{aligned}

<emptyline>
Cost of a cup of tea $= \pounds 1.70$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-33003293-7D63-4EEE-98E2-B0957B77805A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The difference between a third of a certain number and one quarter of the number is $5$. What is the number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {3} - \dfrac {a} {4}                                     &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3} &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {4a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12}                                 &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {4a - 3a} {12}                                               &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                                                     &= 5 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {3} - \dfrac {a} {4}                                     &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3} &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {4a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12}                                 &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {4a - 3a} {12}                                               &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                                                     &= 5 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-1B3F4C98-47C5-4822-9812-B9AD2C131A7A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find $n$ if $126 \div n = 9$

- $14$
- $16$
- $17$
- $11$
- $13$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
126 \div n          &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac {126} {n}    &= 9 \\\\
126                 &= 9n \\\\
9n                  &= 126 \\\\
n                   &= \dfrac {126} {9} \\\\
n                   &= \dfrac {9 \times 14} {9} \\\\
n                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 14} {\cancel {9}} \\\\
n                   &= 14
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
126 \div n          &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac {126} {n}    &= 9 \\\\
126                 &= 9n \\\\
9n                  &= 126 \\\\
n                   &= \dfrac {126} {9} \\\\
n                   &= \dfrac {9 \times 14} {9} \\\\
n                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 14} {\cancel {9}} \\\\
n                   &= 14
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-072C52DE-5BD3-4299-845A-4CA76E632B3F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Simplify the expression $4c - 6d + 2c + 3d$.

- $6c - 3d$
- $15cd$
- $2c + 9d$
- $7c + 8d$
- $6c - 9d$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 4c - 6d + 2c + 3d \\\\
&= 4c  + 2c - 6d + 3d \\\\
&= 6c - 6d + 3d \\\\
&= 6c - 3d \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$6c - 3d$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 4c - 6d + 2c + 3d \\\\
&= 4c  + 2c - 6d + 3d \\\\
&= 6c - 6d + 3d \\\\
&= 6c - 3d \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6c - 3d$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-85473E1B-41B4-4C2F-8183-407C3B81B2F5'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$P = 9a - 7b^{2}$

Work out the value of $P$ when $a = 3$ and $b = -2$.

- $14$
- $-169$
- $3$
- $55$
- $-1$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
P   &= 9a - 7b^2 \\\\
P   &= 9 \times 3 - 7 \times (-2)^2 \\\\
P   &= 27 - 7 \times 4 \\\\
P   &= 27 - 28 \\\\
P   &= -1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>E</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$-1$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
P   &= 9a - 7b^2 \\\\
P   &= 9 \times 3 - 7 \times (-2)^2 \\\\
P   &= 27 - 7 \times 4 \\\\
P   &= 27 - 28 \\\\
P   &= -1
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>E</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$-1$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B4811CE8-001C-4E89-B5D6-B3377D1B9012'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A small ink cartridge has enough ink to print $400$ pages. 
Three small cartridges can print as many pages as two medium cartridges. 
Three medium cartridges can print as many pages as two large cartridges. 
How many pages can be printed using a large cartridge?

- $600$
- $900$
- $1200$
- $1800$
- $2400$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let small cartridge $= s$

Let medium cartridge $= m$

Let large cartridge $= l$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
s   &= 400 \ \text {pages} \\\\
2m  &= 3s \\\\
2m  &= 3 \times 400 \\\\
2m  &= 1200 \\\\
m   &= \dfrac {1200} {2} \\\\
m   &= \dfrac {2 \times 600} {2} \\\\
m   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 600} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
m   &= 600 \ \text {pages} \\\\
2l  &= 3m \\\\
2l  &= 3 \times 600 \\\\
2l  &= 1800 \\\\
l   &= \dfrac {1800} {2} \\\\
l   &= \dfrac {2 \times 900} {2} \\\\
l   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 900} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
l   &= 900 \ \text {pages}
\end{aligned}
$

A large cartridge can print $= 900 \ \text {pages}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$900$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let small cartridge $= s$
<emptyline>
Let medium cartridge $= m$
<emptyline>
Let large cartridge $= l$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
s   &= 400 \ \text {pages} \\\\
2m  &= 3s \\\\
2m  &= 3 \times 400 \\\\
2m  &= 1200 \\\\
m   &= \dfrac {1200} {2} \\\\
m   &= \dfrac {2 \times 600} {2} \\\\
m   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 600} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
m   &= 600 \ \text {pages} \\\\
2l  &= 3m \\\\
2l  &= 3 \times 600 \\\\
2l  &= 1800 \\\\
l   &= \dfrac {1800} {2} \\\\
l   &= \dfrac {2 \times 900} {2} \\\\
l   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 900} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
l   &= 900 \ \text {pages}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
A large cartridge can print $= 900 \ \text {pages}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$900$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F76B40C9-E94E-4682-9F9E-D4DDAFE42591'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Khris Krigle gives each of his helper’s one twelfth of a giant chocolate bar that he had. 
One third of the bar was left. How many helpers did he have?

- $6$
- $8$
- $12$
- $15$
- $18$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of helpers $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 - a \times \dfrac {1} {12}    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
1 - \dfrac {a} {12}             &= \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
1 - \dfrac {1} {3}              &= \dfrac {a} {12} \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                 &= 1 - \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                 &= \dfrac {1 \times 3} {1 \times 3} - \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                 &= \dfrac {3} {3} - \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                 &= \dfrac {3 - 1} {3} \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                 &= \dfrac {2} {3} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 12} {3} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 4} {3} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {3} \times 4} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                               &= 2 \times 4 \\\\
a                               &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

Number of helpers $= 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of helpers $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
1 - a \times \dfrac {1} {12}    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
1 - \dfrac {a} {12}             &= \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
1 - \dfrac {1} {3}              &= \dfrac {a} {12} \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                 &= 1 - \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                 &= \dfrac {1 \times 3} {1 \times 3} - \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                 &= \dfrac {3} {3} - \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                 &= \dfrac {3 - 1} {3} \\\\
\dfrac {a} {12}                 &= \dfrac {2} {3} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 12} {3} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 4} {3} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {3} \times 4} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                               &= 2 \times 4 \\\\
a                               &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of helpers $= 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-51FA34A0-EEA9-468D-9DD3-11BB4CBBF4D0'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Two numbers $X$ and $Y$ (where $Y$ is bigger than $X$), have a Highest Common Factor of $X$. 
What is the Lowest Common Multiple of $X$ and $Y$?
- $Y$
- $X2$
- $Y2$
- $2Y$
- $XY$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


X must be in included LCM.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{LCM}          &= X \times \dfrac{Y}{X} \\\\
                    &= \cancel{X} \times \dfrac{Y}{\cancel{X}} \\\\
                    &= Y
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$Y$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
X must be in included LCM.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{LCM}          &= X \times \dfrac{Y}{X} \\\\
                    &= \cancel{X} \times \dfrac{Y}{\cancel{X}} \\\\
                    &= Y
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$Y$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-ED008E3F-8F7E-4C9F-9A58-76EFAE55003D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

What is the missing number?

$560 \div \boxed{ \phantom{70} } = 8$

- $7$
- $70$
- $700$
- $0.7$
- $0.007$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
560 \div a          &= 8 \\\\
\dfrac{560} {a}     &= 8 \\\\
560                 &= 8a \\\\
8a                  &= 560 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {560} {8} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 70} {8} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 70} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a                   &= 70
\end{aligned}
$

Missing number $= 70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
560 \div a          &= 8 \\\\
\dfrac{560} {a}     &= 8 \\\\
560                 &= 8a \\\\
8a                  &= 560 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {560} {8} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {8 \times 70} {8} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {8} \times 70} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
a                   &= 70
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Missing number $= 70$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$70$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8F65EFC2-3A6B-4CAA-94E2-F2E6C6BB69F5'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

I think of a number, multiply by $2$ and add $5$. The answer is $19$. What was the number?

- $6$
- $8$
- $7$
- $48$
- $28$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 5  &= 19 \\\\
2a              &= 19 - 5 \\\\
2a              &= 14 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{14} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {2 \times 7} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 7} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a               &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 5  &= 19 \\\\
2a              &= 19 - 5 \\\\
2a              &= 14 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac{14} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {2 \times 7} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 7} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a               &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C2CBB803-CAE6-49B9-ADCD-2636CA8618C1'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$4d + 7e - d + 2e =$

- $3d - 9e$
- $12de$
- $4d - 9e$
- $3d + 9e$
- $5d - 9e$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned} 
&=  4d + 7e - d + 2e \\\\
&=  4d -d + 7e + 2e \\\\
&=  3d + 9e
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$3d + 9e$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned} 
&=  4d + 7e - d + 2e \\\\
&=  4d -d + 7e + 2e \\\\
&=  3d + 9e
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>D</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3d + 9e$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8F7E2B54-63AD-42F0-AD22-0B1036DA619C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$C = 5W + 10A$

Work out the value of $C$ when $W = 3$ and $A = -1$

- $14$
- $45$
- $5$
- $25$
- $17$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
C   &=  5W +10A \\\\
C   &=  5 \times 3 + 10 \times -1 \\\\
C   &=  15 - 10 \\\\
C   &=  5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
C   &=  5W +10A \\\\
C   &=  5 \times 3 + 10 \times -1 \\\\
C   &=  15 - 10 \\\\
C   &=  5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-03062397-6A4C-462F-A913-57F1FA0E1B4A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

When my age is divided by $2, 3, 4$ or $6$ there is always a remainder of $1$. 
But when divided by $7$ there is no remainder.
How old am I?

- $14$
- $49$ 
- $7$  
- $21$  
- $35$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Lcm of 2,3,4,6 = 12

Let the number $= 12n + 1$

As per the question,

$12n + 1$ should be divisible by $7$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Lets find out the value of n which satisfies the condition above.

| n |  12n + 1               |  Divisible by 7 |
|:-:|:----------------------:|:---------------:|
|$1$| $12 \times 1 + 1 = 13$ |        No       |
|$2$| $12 \times 2 + 1 = 10$ |        Yes      |
|$3$| $12 \times 3 + 1 = 37$ |        No       |
|$4$| $12 \times 4 + 1 = 49$ |        Yes      |

A quick check shows that $n = 4$ meets all the criteria above.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number} &= 12 \times n + 1 \\\\
              &= 12 \times 4 + 1 \\\\
              &= 48 + 1 \\\\
              &= 49
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$49$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Lcm of 2,3,4,6 = 12
<emptyline>
Let the number $= 12n + 1$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$12n + 1$ should be divisible by $7$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Lets find out the value of n which satisfies the condition above.
<emptyline>
| n |  12n + 1               |  Divisible by 7 |
|:-:|:----------------------:|:---------------:|
|$1$| $12 \times 1 + 1 = 13$ |        No       |
|$2$| $12 \times 2 + 1 = 10$ |        Yes      |
|$3$| $12 \times 3 + 1 = 37$ |        No       |
|$4$| $12 \times 4 + 1 = 49$ |        Yes      |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $n = 4$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number} &= 12 \times n + 1 \\\\
              &= 12 \times 4 + 1 \\\\
              &= 48 + 1 \\\\
              &= 49
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$49$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-85EB3724-4385-40C7-99FC-BD96F06DD4F3'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Think of two integers that have a product of $18$ and a difference 
that is the same as one of the two integers that you are thinking of. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $= a$

Let the second number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b            &= 18 \\\\
a - b                 &= b \\\\
a                     &= 2 \times b
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve them. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.

Substituting the value of $a$ from second equation to first equation,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b            &= 18 \\\\
2 \times b \times b   &= 18 \\\\
2 b^2                 &= 18 \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {18} {2} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 9} {2} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 9} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= 9 \\\\
b                     &= \sqrt {9} \\\\
b                     &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

Substituting the value of $b$ in second equation.

$
\begin{aligned}
a - b &= b \\\\
a - 3 &= 3 \\\\
a     &= 3 + 3 \\\\
a     &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

The two integers are $3$ and $6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
Substituting the value of $a$ from second equation to first equation,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

2 \times b \times b   &= 18 \\\\
2 b^2                 &= 18 \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {18} {2} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 9} {2} \\\\
b ^ 2                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 9} {\cancel {2}} \\\\

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Substituting the value of $b$ in second equation.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

a     &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The two integers are $3$ and $6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A44515A9-1C0B-4A2E-99CA-D73054BD487E'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Sam thinks of a number. 
If you double the number and add $6$ you get the same answer as multiplying it by $3$ and subtracting $3$.

Find the number that Sam was thinking of. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number sam thought of $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3a - 3  &= 2a + 6 \\\\
3a - 2a &= 6 + 3 \\\\
a       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

The number Sam thought of $= 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number sam thought of $=a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3a - 3  &= 2a + 6 \\\\
3a - 2a &= 6 + 3 \\\\
a       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number Sam thought of $= 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-83315ED9-DB65-48DC-A17E-D2511B4529A7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Three friends went to a burger restaurant.

Tom had a burger and chips and he paid $\pounds 8.00$.

Fred had two burgers and a drink. Fred paid $\pounds 15.00$.

Jo had a burger and a drink. Jo paid $\pounds 8.50$.

Work out the cost of the burger, the chips and the drink. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of a burger $= b$

Let the cost of chips $= c$

Let the cost of a drink $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Tom paid}    &= \pounds 8 \\\\
b + c               &= 8 \\\\
\text {Fred paid}   &= \pounds 15 \\\\
2b + d              &= 15 \\\\
\text {Jo paid}     &= \pounds 8.50 \\\\
b + d               &= 8.50
\end{aligned}
$

Using the above two equations we get,

$
\begin{aligned} 
2b + d - b - d  &= 15 - 8.50 \\\\
b               &= \pounds 6.50
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of the burger $= \pounds 6.50$

$
\begin {aligned}
b + c       &= 8 \\\\
6.50 + c    &= 8 \\\\
c           &= 8 - 6.50 \\\\
c           &= \pounds 1.50
\end {aligned}
$

Cost of the chips $= \pounds 1.50$

$
\begin {aligned}
b + d       &= 8.50 \\\\
6.50 + d    &= 8.50 \\\\
d           &= 8.50 - 6.50 \\\\
d           &= \pounds 2
\end {aligned}
$

Cost of the drink $= \pounds 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Burger $=$ $\pounds 6.50$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Chips $=$ $\pounds 1.50$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Drink $=$ $\pounds 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of a burger $= b$
<emptyline>
Let the cost of chips $= c$

$

\end {aligned}
$

$

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-226E8E2D-19C1-4607-A921-E9F025A6322B'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

In a Maths test Freddie gets $37$ more marks than George. If Freddie gets $61$, what mark does George get?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let George's marks $= a$

Freddie's marks $= a + 37$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 37      &= 61 \\\\
a           &= 61 - 37 \\\\
a           &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

George's marks $= 24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let George's marks $= a$
<emptyline>
Freddie's marks $= a + 37$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 37      &= 61 \\\\
a           &= 61 - 37 \\\\
a           &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
George's marks $= 24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DACF41B7-F660-4A37-8522-2D3B2583F3CA'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

I think of a number, double it, take the answer away from $41$, and I am left with $3$. What number did I think of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
41 - 2a         &= 3 \\\\
41 - 3          &= 2a \\\\
2a              &= 41 - 3 \\\\
2a              &= 38 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {38} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {2 \times 19} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 19} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a               &= 19
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
41 - 2a         &= 3 \\\\
41 - 3          &= 2a \\\\
2a              &= 41 - 3 \\\\
2a              &= 38 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {38} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {2 \times 19} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 19} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a               &= 19
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-4F9F9262-A677-46E7-BF67-C0017CAB35B7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A large crate holds twelve cartons of milk whereas a small crate holds only four cartons. 
I have five large crates and twenty small crates available. 
What is the smallest number of crates in total that I could use to store $110$ cartons of milk?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

To find the smallest amount of crates needed, we have to use all the large crates and based on that calculate how many small crates are needed.

Let the count of small crates $= s$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times 12 + s \times 4    &= 110 \\\\ 
60  + s \times 4            & = 110 \\\\
4s                          & = 110 - 60 \\\\
4s                          & = 50 \\\\
s                           & = \dfrac {48 + 2} {4} \\\\
s                           & = \dfrac {48} {4} + \dfrac {2} {4} \\\\
s                           & = \dfrac {50} {4} \\\\
s                           & = 12 \ \text {remainder } 4 \\\\
s                           &\approx 13 \ \text{crates}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest number of crates needed} &= 13 + 5 \\\\
                                        &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>To find the smallest amount of crates needed, we have to use all the large crates and based on that calculate how many small crates are needed.
<emptyline>
Let the count of small crates $= s$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times 12 + s \times 4    &= 110 \\\\ 
60  + s \times 4            & = 110 \\\\
4s                          & = 110 - 60 \\\\
4s                          & = 50 \\\\
s                           & = \dfrac {48 + 2} {4} \\\\
s                           & = \dfrac {48} {4} + \dfrac {2} {4} \\\\
s                           & = \dfrac {50} {4} \\\\
s                           & = 12 \ \text {remainder } 4 \\\\
s                           &\approx 13 \ \text{crates}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest number of crates needed} &= 13 + 5 \\\\
                                        &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


<div class='review'>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7F32C156-E6CA-40AB-9A96-8977312C93E4'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Ravi has two sisters, Harsha and Harpreet. He likes to share out his sweets unfairly. 
One day he gives Harsha twice as many as he gives himself, and he gives Harpreet half 
as many as he gives himself. 
If there are $42$ sweets in total how many does Ravi get?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of sweets Ravi got $= a$

Number of sweets Harsha got $= 2a$

Number of sweets Harpreet got $= \dfrac {a} {2}$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 2a + \dfrac {a} {2}     &= 42 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 2} {1 \times 2} + \dfrac {2a \times 2} {1 \times 2} + \dfrac {a} {2}   &= 42 \\\\
\dfrac {2a} {2} + \dfrac {4a} {2} + \dfrac {a} {2}                                      &= 42 \\\\
\dfrac {2a + 4a + a} {2}                                                                &= 42 \\\\
\dfrac {7a} {2}                                                                         &= 42 \\\\
7a                                                                                      &= 42 \times 2 \\\\
a                                                                                       &= \dfrac {42 \times 2} {7} \\\\
a                                                                                       &= \dfrac {6 \times 7 \times 2} {7} \\\\
a                                                                                       &= \dfrac {6 \times \cancel {7} \times 2} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
a                                                                                       &= 6 \times 2 \\\\
a                                                                                       &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

Number of sweets Ravi got $= 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of sweets Ravi got $= a$

\begin{aligned}

\dfrac {2a} {2} + \dfrac {4a} {2} + \dfrac {a} {2}                                      &= 42 \\\\
\dfrac {2a + 4a + a} {2}                                                                &= 42 \\\\
\dfrac {7a} {2}                                                                         &= 42 \\\\
7a                                                                                      &= 42 \times 2 \\\\
a                                                                                       &= \dfrac {42 \times 2} {7} \\\\
a                                                                                       &= \dfrac {6 \times 7 \times 2} {7} \\\\
a                                                                                       &= \dfrac {6 \times \cancel {7} \times 2} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
a                                                                                       &= 6 \times 2 \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-3250EBF1-2D1A-48CA-B1EA-DF0B51214B2E'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A person's Body Mass Index $(BMI$) is worked out by dividing their weight (in kilograms) by the square of their height (in metres). A person is then classified as "underweight", "normal", "overweight" or "obese" according to the following table:

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

For example, Jill weighs $72 \ \text{kg}$ and is $1.82 \ \text{m}$ tall. Her body mass index is worked out as follows:

$BMI = \dfrac{72}{1.82^2} = 21.7$ which is between $18.6$ and $24.9$ so Jill is "normal".

At the bottom of this page you will find the results of lots of calculations, some of which are relevant to the questions below. 
The one that has been circled was used to find Jill's $BMI$.



</div>

\text {Lowest weight Josephine need to put on} &= 61.6 - 53 \\\\
                                               &= 8.6 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-245B324A-6DAB-4088-BFC8-09EDBB1D03E1'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Look carefully at the following five supermarket offers:

- Buy one get one free.

- Buy one get the second for half price.

- Buy two get one free.

- Buy four and pay only for three.

- One-third off all prices.

Two pairs of these offers actually give the same value for money. Write down the letters representing both of these pairs in the spaces below:

$\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}$ and $\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}$ give the same value for money, and

$\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}$ and $\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}$ give the same value for money.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let's take the original price $=a$

Now let's find the number of items one gets in each of the offer and the money one has to pay for it.

**Offer A : Buy one get one free.** 

Items $=2$ 

  Price $=a$

$
\begin{aligned} 
    \text{Item}                   & : \text{Price}   \\\\
    2                             & : a             \\\\
    \dfrac {2}{2}                 & : \dfrac {a}{2} \\\\
    \dfrac {\cancel 2}{\cancel 2} & : \dfrac {a}{2} \\\\
    1                             & : \dfrac {a}{2} 
\end{aligned}
$

**Offer B : Buy one get the second for half price.** 

Items $=2$ 

  Price $=a + \dfrac{a}{2}$

$
\begin{aligned} 
    \text{Item}                   & : \text{Price}                                    \\\\
    2                             & : a + \dfrac {a}{2}                               \\\\
    2                             & : \dfrac {a}{1} + \dfrac {a}{2}                   \\\\
    2                             & : \dfrac {a \times 2}{1 \times 2} + \dfrac {a}{2} \\\\
    2 \times \dfrac{1}{2}         & : \dfrac {2a  + a} {2} \times  \dfrac{1}{2}       \\\\
    \dfrac {\cancel 2}{\cancel 2} & : \dfrac {3a} {4}                                 \\\\
    1                             & : \dfrac {3a}{4}                                  
\end{aligned}
$


**Offer C : Buy two get one free.** 

Items $=3$ 

  Price $=2a$

$
\begin{aligned} 
    \text{Item}                   & : \text{Price}       \\\\
    3                             & : 2a                \\\\
    \dfrac {3}{3}                 & : \dfrac {2a}{3}    \\\\
    \dfrac {\cancel 3}{\cancel 3} & : \dfrac {2a}{3}    \\\\
    1                             & : \dfrac {2a}{3} 
\end{aligned}
$


**Offer D : Buy four and pay only for three.** 

Items $=4$ 

  Price $=3a$

$
\begin{aligned} 
    \text{Item}                   & : \text{Price}     \\\\
    4                             & : 3a              \\\\
    \dfrac {4}{4}                 & : \dfrac {3a} {4} \\\\    
    \dfrac {\cancel 4}{\cancel 4} & : \dfrac {3a} {4} \\\\
    1                             & : \dfrac {3a}{4}  
\end{aligned}
$


**Offer E : One-third off all prices** 

Items $=1$ 

  Pay $=a - \dfrac {1}{3}$


$
\begin{aligned} 
    \text{Item} & : \text{Price}                                    \\\\
    1           & : a - \dfrac {1}{3}                               \\\\
    1           & : \dfrac {a}{1} - \dfrac {1}{3}                   \\\\
    1           & : \dfrac {a \times 3}{1 \times 3} - \dfrac {1}{3} \\\\
    1           & : \dfrac {3a} {3} -  \dfrac{1}{3}                 \\\\
    1           & : \dfrac {3a - 1} {3}                             \\\\
    1           & : \dfrac {2a}{3}                                  
\end{aligned}
$

Ratio of one item cost under the offers:

$
\begin{aligned} 
\text {Offer A} &= \dfrac {a}{2} \\\\
\text {Offer B} &= \dfrac {3a}{4} \\\\
\text {Offer C} &= \dfrac {2a}{3}  \\\\
\text {Offer D} &= \dfrac {3a}{4} \\\\
\text {Offer E} &= \dfrac {2a}{3}
\end{aligned}
$

As we can see above,

$B = D$ and $C = E$

$\underline {\text {B}}$ and  $\underline {\text {D}}$ give the same value for money, and

$\underline {\text {C}}$ and  $\underline {\text {E}}$ give the same value for money.



</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\underline {\text {B}}$ and  $\underline {\text {D}}$ give the same value for money, and

$\underline {\text {C}}$ and  $\underline {\text {E}}$ give the same value for money.

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let's take the original price $=a$
<emptyline>
Now let's find the number of items one gets in each of the offer and the money one has to pay for it.
<emptyline>
**Offer A : Buy one get one free.** 
<emptyline>
Items $=2$ 
<emptyline>
  Price $=a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned} 
    \text{Item}                   & : \text{Price}   \\\\
    2                             & : a             \\\\
    \dfrac {2}{2}                 & : \dfrac {a}{2} \\\\
    \dfrac {\cancel 2}{\cancel 2} & : \dfrac {a}{2} \\\\
    1                             & : \dfrac {a}{2} 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
**Offer B : Buy one get the second for half price.** 
<emptyline>
Items $=2$ 
<emptyline>
  Price $=a + \dfrac{a}{2}$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned} 
    \text{Item}                   & : \text{Price}                                    \\\\
    2                             & : a + \dfrac {a}{2}                               \\\\
    2                             & : \dfrac {a}{1} + \dfrac {a}{2}                   \\\\
    2                             & : \dfrac {a \times 2}{1 \times 2} + \dfrac {a}{2} \\\\
    2 \times \dfrac{1}{2}         & : \dfrac {2a  + a} {2} \times  \dfrac{1}{2}       \\\\
    \dfrac {\cancel 2}{\cancel 2} & : \dfrac {3a} {4}                                 \\\\
    1                             & : \dfrac {3a}{4}                                  
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

**Offer C : Buy two get one free.** 
<emptyline>
Items $=3$ 
<emptyline>
  Price $=2a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned} 
    \text{Item}                   & : \text{Price}       \\\\
    3                             & : 2a                \\\\
    \dfrac {3}{3}                 & : \dfrac {2a}{3}    \\\\
    \dfrac {\cancel 3}{\cancel 3} & : \dfrac {2a}{3}    \\\\
    1                             & : \dfrac {2a}{3} 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

**Offer D : Buy four and pay only for three.** 
<emptyline>
Items $=4$ 
<emptyline>
  Price $=3a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned} 
    \text{Item}                   & : \text{Price}     \\\\
    4                             & : 3a              \\\\
    \dfrac {4}{4}                 & : \dfrac {3a} {4} \\\\    
    \dfrac {\cancel 4}{\cancel 4} & : \dfrac {3a} {4} \\\\
    1                             & : \dfrac {3a}{4}  
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

**Offer E : One-third off all prices** 
<emptyline>
Items $=1$ 
<emptyline>
  Pay $=a - \dfrac {1}{3}$
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned} 
    \text{Item} & : \text{Price}                                    \\\\
    1           & : a - \dfrac {1}{3}                               \\\\
    1           & : \dfrac {a}{1} - \dfrac {1}{3}                   \\\\
    1           & : \dfrac {a \times 3}{1 \times 3} - \dfrac {1}{3} \\\\
    1           & : \dfrac {3a} {3} -  \dfrac{1}{3}                 \\\\
    1           & : \dfrac {3a - 1} {3}                             \\\\
    1           & : \dfrac {2a}{3}                                  
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Ratio of one item cost under the offers:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned} 
\text {Offer A} &= \dfrac {a}{2} \\\\
\text {Offer B} &= \dfrac {3a}{4} \\\\
\text {Offer C} &= \dfrac {2a}{3}  \\\\
\text {Offer D} &= \dfrac {3a}{4} \\\\
\text {Offer E} &= \dfrac {2a}{3}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As we can see above,
<emptyline>
$B = D$ and $C = E$
<emptyline>
$\underline {\text {B}}$ and  $\underline {\text {D}}$ give the same value for money, and
<emptyline>
$\underline {\text {C}}$ and  $\underline {\text {E}}$ give the same value for money.
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\underline {\text {B}}$ and  $\underline {\text {D}}$ give the same value for money, and

$\underline {\text {C}}$ and  $\underline {\text {E}}$ give the same value for money.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-821275C1-8DB4-4E05-91B4-87F51A34EDD4'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Four friends, Alwyn, Bax, Coates and Delius have lunch together. Although each eats a differently priced snack, 
they share the bill equally. Yesterday, this resulted in Alwyn paying $\pounds 1$ less than the cost of his snack, 
Bax paying $\pounds 3$ more, Coates $\pounds 4$ less and Delius paid $\pounds 4$. 
What was the actual price of the snack that each friend ordered?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

All four friends shared the bill equally. 

Delius paid $\pounds 4$. So, all four friends paid $\pounds 4$ each.

$
\begin{aligned}
Alwyn snack cost      &= 4 + 1 \\\\
                      &= \pounds 5 \\\\
Bax snack cost        &= 4 - 3 \\\\
                      &= \pounds 1 \\\\
Coates snack cost     &= 4 + 4 \\\\
                      &= \pounds 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5,1,8,4$

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
All four friends shared the bill equally. 
<emptyline>
Delius paid $\pounds 4$. So, all four friends paid $\pounds 4$ each.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
Alwyn snack cost      &= 4 + 1 \\\\
                      &= \pounds 5 \\\\
Bax snack cost        &= 4 - 3 \\\\
                      &= \pounds 1 \\\\
Coates snack cost     &= 4 + 4 \\\\
                      &= \pounds 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5,1,8,4$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-4F789E2C-F431-4D0D-81F6-47D727DDF8FA'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

An orange costs twice as much as an apple. Which one of the following does not cost the same as the other four?

- Two oranges and three apples

- Seven apples

- One orange and five apples

- Three oranges and one apple

- Four oranges

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of apple $= a$

Cost of orange $= 2a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of Two oranges and three apples}     &= 2 \times 2a + 3 \times a \\\\
                                                &= 4a + 3a \\\\
                                                &= 7a \\\\
\text{Cost of seven apples}                     &= 7 \times a \\\\
                                                &= 7a \\\\
\text{Cost of One orange and five apples}       &= 2 \times a + 5 \times a \\\\
                                                &= 2a + 5a \\\\
                                                &= 7a \\\\
\text{Cost of Three oranges and one apple}      &= 3 \times 2a + a \\\\ 
                                                &= 6a + a \\\\ 
                                                &= 7a \\\\ 
\text{Cost of four oranges}                     &= 4 \times 2a \\\\
                                                &= 8a
\end{aligned}
$

The price of four oranges is not the same as the price of the other four.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>E</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

Four oranges

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of apple $= a$
<emptyline>
Cost of orange $= 2a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of Two oranges and three apples}     &= 2 \times 2a + 3 \times a \\\\
                                                &= 4a + 3a \\\\
                                                &= 7a \\\\
\text{Cost of seven apples}                     &= 7 \times a \\\\
                                                &= 7a \\\\
\text{Cost of One orange and five apples}       &= 2 \times a + 5 \times a \\\\
                                                &= 2a + 5a \\\\
                                                &= 7a \\\\
\text{Cost of Three oranges and one apple}      &= 3 \times 2a + a \\\\ 
                                                &= 6a + a \\\\ 
                                                &= 7a \\\\ 
\text{Cost of four oranges}                     &= 4 \times 2a \\\\
                                                &= 8a
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The price of four oranges is not the same as the price of the other four.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>E</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Four oranges
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long would a team of eight people have taken?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken}  &= \dfrac{4 \times 6}{8} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{24}{8} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{3 \times 8}{8} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{8}}{\cancel{8}} \\\\
                   &= 3 \text{ hours}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \text{ hours}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken}  &= \dfrac{4 \times 6}{8} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{24}{8} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{3 \times 8}{8} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{8}}{\cancel{8}} \\\\
                   &= 3 \text{ hours}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3 \text{ hours}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

One of my neighbours has a similar fence which is twice as long as mine.  She would like the work to be completed in less than five hours.  What is the minimum number of people that she should employ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of employees $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6} {a} &< 5 \\\\
\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6} {a} &< 5 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

|  a   |           Time taken (in hours)           |   Less than 5 hours  |
|:----:|:-----------------------------------------:|:--------------------:|
| $1$  |   $\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6}{1} = 48$   |        No            |
| $2$  |   $\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6}{2} = 24$   |        No            |
| $3$  |   $\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6}{3} = 16$   |        No            |
| $4$  |   $\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6}{4} = 12$   |        No            |
| $5$  |   $\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6}{5} = 9.6$  |        No            |
| $6$  |   $\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6}{6} = 8$    |        No            |
| $7$  |   $\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6}{7} = 6.8$  |        No            |
| $8$  |   $\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6}{8} = 6$    |        No            |
| $9$  |   $\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6}{9} = 5.3$  |        No            |
| $10$ |   $\dfrac{2 \times 4 \times 6}{10} = 4.8$ |        Yes           |

Minimum number of people needed $= 10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of employees $= a$
<emptyline>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-68B5F9D8-6802-4D8A-9E11-07B4B6B7DBB1'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Write down two numbers which differ by $2$ and multiply to $168$. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $= a$

Let the first number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - b           &= 2 \\\\
a \times b      &= 168
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $168$. 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $2$.

Factors of $48 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, 12, 14, 21, 24, 28, 42, 56, 84 \text{ and } 168$

Factor pairs of $48$ that have a difference of $2 = 14,12$

| Factors   |  Product  |    Difference     |
|:-------:  |:--------: |:----------------: |
| $14, 12$  |   $168$   |       $2$         |

A quick check shows $12$ and $14$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers are $= 14 \text {and} 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14, 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the first number $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

a \times b      &= 168
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.
<emptyline>

- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $2$.
<emptyline>
Factors of $48 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, 12, 14, 21, 24, 28, 42, 56, 84 \text{ and } 168$
<emptyline>
Factor pairs of $48$ that have a difference of $2 = 14,12$
<emptyline>
| Factors   |  Product  |    Difference     |
|:-------:  |:--------: |:----------------: |
| $14, 12$  |   $168$   |       $2$         |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows $12$ and $14$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. rtq_abbr_md_quadratic_note*
<emptyline>
The two numbers are $= 14 \text {and} 12$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-0E8B9210-8E51-48CA-8F41-6F60BB08A506'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Suzie is given a number and told to divide it by $2$ and then subtract $14$. 
Although Suzie starts with the correct number she gets in a muddle and multiplies by $2$ and then adds $14$ instead. 
If her final answer is $142$, what answer should she have got?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 14   &= 142 \\\\
2a + 14           &= 142 \\\\
2a                &= 142 - 14 \\\\
2a                &= 128 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{128}{2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{64 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{64 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                 &= 64
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Answer Suzie could've got }   &= \dfrac{64}{2} - 14 \\\\ 
                                    &= \dfrac{64}{2} - 14 \\\\ 
                                    &= \dfrac{32 \times 2}{2} - 14 \\\\ 
                                    &= \dfrac{32 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} - 14 \\\\ 
                                    &= 32 - 14 \\\\ 
                                    &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 14   &= 142 \\\\
2a + 14           &= 142 \\\\
2a                &= 142 - 14 \\\\
2a                &= 128 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{128}{2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{64 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{64 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                 &= 64
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Answer Suzie could've got }   &= \dfrac{64}{2} - 14 \\\\ 
                                    &= \dfrac{64}{2} - 14 \\\\ 
                                    &= \dfrac{32 \times 2}{2} - 14 \\\\ 
                                    &= \dfrac{32 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} - 14 \\\\ 
                                    &= 32 - 14 \\\\ 
                                    &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F8180122-4CE2-4E5E-A043-7D79F32DDBED'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The cost of $3$ apples, $5$ oranges and $2$ grapefruit is $\pounds 3.02$. 

The cost of $5$ apples, $7$ oranges and $4$ grapefruit is $\pounds 5.12$. 

What is the total cost of one of each type of fruit?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of apples $= a$

and the cost of oranges $= o$

and the cost of grapefruit $= g$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 5o + 2g                                &= 3.02 \\\\
5a + 7o + 4g                                &= 5.12
\end{aligned}
$

Subtracting equation $1$ from equation $2$

$
\begin{aligned}
5a + 7o + 4g - (3a + 5o + 2g)                                                                                           &= 5.12 - 3.02 \\\\
5a - 3a + 7o - 5o + 4g - 2g                                                                                             &= 2.1 \\\\
2a + 2o + 2g                                                                                                            &= 2.1 \\\\
\dfrac{2a + 2o + 2g}{2}                                                                                                 &= \dfrac{2.1}{2} \\\\
\dfrac{2a}{2} + \dfrac{2o}{2} + \dfrac{2g}{2}                                                                           &= \dfrac{1.05 \times 2}{2} \\\\
\dfrac{\cancel{2}a}{\cancel{2}} + \dfrac{\cancel{2}o}{\cancel{2}} + \dfrac{\cancel{2}g}{\cancel{2}}                           &= \dfrac{1.05 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a + o + g                                                                                                               &= \pounds 1.05
\end{aligned}
$

Total cost of one of each type of fruit $= \pounds 1.05$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.05$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of apples $= a$
<emptyline>
and the cost of oranges $= o$
<emptyline>
and the cost of grapefruit $= g$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 5o + 2g                                &= 3.02 \\\\
5a + 7o + 4g                                &= 5.12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Subtracting equation $1$ from equation $2$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5a + 7o + 4g - (3a + 5o + 2g)                                                                                           &= 5.12 - 3.02 \\\\
5a - 3a + 7o - 5o + 4g - 2g                                                                                             &= 2.1 \\\\
2a + 2o + 2g                                                                                                            &= 2.1 \\\\
\dfrac{2a + 2o + 2g}{2}                                                                                                 &= \dfrac{2.1}{2} \\\\
\dfrac{2a}{2} + \dfrac{2o}{2} + \dfrac{2g}{2}                                                                           &= \dfrac{1.05 \times 2}{2} \\\\
\dfrac{\cancel{2}a}{\cancel{2}} + \dfrac{\cancel{2}o}{\cancel{2}} + \dfrac{\cancel{2}g}{\cancel{2}}                           &= \dfrac{1.05 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a + o + g                                                                                                               &= \pounds 1.05
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total cost of one of each type of fruit $= \pounds 1.05$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.05$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B997FB3B-115F-4CC3-BC0E-C9D91855E806'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Children are offered a $25\%$ discount on the cost of an adult ticket to visit Hamshaw House. 
Senior citizens are given a $20\%$ discount. If a child's ticket costs $\pounds 30$, how much does a senior citizen pay?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of an adult ticket $= a$

Cost of a child ticket $= a - 25% of a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 25\% \text{of} a                                                  &= 30 \\\\
a - \dfrac {25} {100} \times a                                        &= 30 \\\\
a - \dfrac {25a} {100}                                                &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac{a \times 100}{1 \times 100} - \dfrac {25a} {100}               &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac{100a}{100} - \dfrac {25a} {100}                                &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac{100a - 25a}{100}                                               &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac{75a}{100}                                                      &= 30 \\\\
75a                                                                   &= 30 \times 100 \\\\
75a                                                                   &= 3000 \\\\
a                                                                     &= \dfrac{3000}{75} \\\\
a                                                                     &= \dfrac{40 \times 75}{75} \\\\
a                                                                     &= \dfrac{40 \times \cancel{75}}{\cancel{75}} \\\\
a                                                                     &= \pounds 40
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Cost of a senior citizen's ticket}   &= \text {Cost of an adult ticket} - 20 \% \ \text {of} \ \text {Cost of an adult ticket} \\\\
                                            &= 40 - \dfrac {20} {100} \times 40 \\\\
                                            &= 40 - \dfrac {20 \times 40} {100} \\\\
                                            &= 40 - \dfrac {20 \times 5 \times 8} {20 \times 5} \\\\
                                            &= 40 - \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times \cancel {5} \times 8} {\cancel {20} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                                            &= 40 - 8 \\\\
                                            &= \pounds 32
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of an adult ticket $= a$
<emptyline>
Cost of a child ticket $= a - 25% of a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a - 25\% \text{of} a                                                  &= 30 \\\\
a - \dfrac {25} {100} \times a                                        &= 30 \\\\
a - \dfrac {25a} {100}                                                &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac{a \times 100}{1 \times 100} - \dfrac {25a} {100}               &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac{100a}{100} - \dfrac {25a} {100}                                &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac{100a - 25a}{100}                                               &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac{75a}{100}                                                      &= 30 \\\\
75a                                                                   &= 30 \times 100 \\\\
75a                                                                   &= 3000 \\\\
a                                                                     &= \dfrac{3000}{75} \\\\
a                                                                     &= \dfrac{40 \times 75}{75} \\\\
a                                                                     &= \dfrac{40 \times \cancel{75}}{\cancel{75}} \\\\
a                                                                     &= \pounds 40
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Cost of a senior citizen's ticket}   &= \text {Cost of an adult ticket} - 20 \% \ \text {of} \ \text {Cost of an adult ticket} \\\\
                                            &= 40 - \dfrac {20} {100} \times 40 \\\\
                                            &= 40 - \dfrac {20 \times 40} {100} \\\\
                                            &= 40 - \dfrac {20 \times 5 \times 8} {20 \times 5} \\\\
                                            &= 40 - \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times \cancel {5} \times 8} {\cancel {20} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                                            &= 40 - 8 \\\\
                                            &= \pounds 32
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 32$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F1D61AFD-09B1-495E-8B01-6F4566287548'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

For every three strawberry jellies in a bag of sweets there are four cherry jellies. 
If a large bag contains $12$ strawberry jellies how many cherry jellies are there in the bag?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The number of cherry jellies in the bag}    &= \dfrac{12 \times 4}{3} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac{12 \times 4}{3} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac{4 \times 3 \times 4}{3} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac{4 \times \cancel{3} \times 4}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
                                                  &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                                                  &= 16 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The number of cherry jellies in the bag}    &= \dfrac{12 \times 4}{3} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac{12 \times 4}{3} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac{4 \times 3 \times 4}{3} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac{4 \times \cancel{3} \times 4}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
                                                  &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                                                  &= 16 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$16$
</code></pre>
</div>
