<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-28B9A7A2-B177-4AA0-8083-661A59F79C19'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Five Whatsits and four Thingummyjigs cost $\pounds 14.95$. Work out the cost of a Whatsit in each of the following cases: 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of Whatsits $= a$

Cost of Thingummyjigs $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5a + 4b       &= 14.95
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cost of Whatsits $= a$
<emptyline>
Cost of Thingummyjigs $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5a + 4b       &= 14.95
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

When twelve Thingummyjigs cost $\pounds 10.20$. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
12b                                                       &= 10.20 \\\\
\dfrac{12b}{3}                                            &= \dfrac{10.20}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{4 \times 3 \times b}{3}                            &= \dfrac{3.4 \times 3}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{4 \times \cancel{3} \times b}{\cancel{3}}          &= \dfrac{3.4 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
4b                                                        &= 3.4
\end{aligned}
$

Let's substitute the value of $4b$ in first equation.

$
\begin{aligned}
5a + 4b           &= 14.95 \\\\
5a + 3.4          &= 14.95 \\\\
5a                &= 11.55 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{11.55}{5} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{2.31 \times 5}{5} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{2.31 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{5}} \\\\
a                 &= \pounds 2.31 \\\\
4b                &= 3.4 \\\\ 
b                 &= \dfrac{0.85 \times 4}{4} \\\\ 
b                 &= \dfrac{0.85 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\ 
b                 &= \pounds 0.85
\end{aligned}
$

The cost of Whatsits $= \pounds 2.31$

The cost of Thingummyjigs $= \pounds 0.85$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 2.31$ and $\pounds 0.85$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
12b                                                       &= 10.20 \\\\
\dfrac{12b}{3}                                            &= \dfrac{10.20}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{4 \times 3 \times b}{3}                            &= \dfrac{3.4 \times 3}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{4 \times \cancel{3} \times b}{\cancel{3}}          &= \dfrac{3.4 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
4b                                                        &= 3.4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's substitute the value of $4b$ in first equation.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5a + 4b           &= 14.95 \\\\
5a + 3.4          &= 14.95 \\\\
5a                &= 11.55 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{11.55}{5} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{2.31 \times 5}{5} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{2.31 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{5}} \\\\
a                 &= \pounds 2.31 \\\\
4b                &= 3.4 \\\\ 
b                 &= \dfrac{0.85 \times 4}{4} \\\\ 
b                 &= \dfrac{0.85 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\ 
b                 &= \pounds 0.85
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The cost of Whatsits $= \pounds 2.31$
<emptyline>
The cost of Thingummyjigs $= \pounds 0.85$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 2.31$ and $\pounds 0.85$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

When a Thingummyjig costs twice as much as a Whatsit.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$b = 2a$

Let's substitute the value of $b$ in first equation.

$
\begin{aligned}
5a + 4b                    &= 14.95 \\\\
5a + 4 \times 2a           &= 14.95 \\\\
5a + 8a                    &= 14.95 \\\\
13a                        &= 14.95 \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{14.95}{13} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{1.15 \times 13}{13} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{1.15 \times \cancel{13}}{\cancel{13}} \\\\
a                          &= \pounds 1.15 \\\\
b                          &= 2a \\\\
b                          &= 2 \times 1.15 \\\\
b                          &= \pounds 2.3
\end{aligned}
$

The cost of Whatsits $= \pounds 1.15$

The cost of Thingummyjigs $= \pounds 2.3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.15$ and $\pounds 2.3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$b = 2a$
<emptyline>
Let's substitute the value of $b$ in first equation.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5a + 4b                    &= 14.95 \\\\
5a + 4 \times 2a           &= 14.95 \\\\

13a                        &= 14.95 \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{14.95}{13} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{1.15 \times 13}{13} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{1.15 \times \cancel{13}}{\cancel{13}} \\\\
a                          &= \pounds 1.15 \\\\
b                          &= 2a \\\\
b                          &= 2 \times 1.15 \\\\
b                          &= \pounds 2.3
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The cost of Whatsits $= \pounds 1.15$
<emptyline>
The cost of Thingummyjigs $= \pounds 2.3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.15$ and $\pounds 2.3$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-651FA161-719C-4CEA-8415-BD9C2BEF8759'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Pens cost $7 \text{p}$ each and pencils cost $5 \text{p}$ each.  I buy some pens and pencils which cost $55 \text{p}$ exactly.

How many of each did I buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of pens $= a$

and the number of pencils $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
7a + 5b       &= 55
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out the value of a and b which satisfies the condition above.

| $a$   |  $b$  |  $7a + 5b$  |
|:-----:|:-----:|------------:|
|$4$    | $6$   |   58        |
|$4$    | $5$   |   53        |
|$5$    | $4$   |   55        |

A quick check shows that $a = 5$ and $b = 4$ meets all the criteria above.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pens}       &= 5 \\\\
\text{Number of pencils}    &= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$ pens and $4$ pencils

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of pens $= a$
<emptyline>
and the number of pencils $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
7a + 5b       &= 55
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out the value of a and b which satisfies the condition above.
<emptyline>
| $a$   |  $b$  |  $7a + 5b$  |
|:-----:|:-----:|------------:|
|$4$    | $6$   |   58        |
|$4$    | $5$   |   53        |
|$5$    | $4$   |   55        |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $a = 5$ and $b = 4$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pens}       &= 5 \\\\
\text{Number of pencils}    &= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$ pens and $4$ pencils
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-12D28059-3C99-4BDF-BF66-584ACAC9EA71'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Mrs Catchpole gives the following instructions to her Year $6$ class:

-  Think of a number. 
-  Subtract $13$ from this number to get the first result. 
-  Go back to the original number and subtract 5 to get the second result. 
-  Multiply the first and second results together to get the final answer.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Savan follows Mrs Catchpole's instructions starting with the number $23$. Work out Savan's final answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (23 - 13) \times (23 - 5) \\\\
&= (23 - 13) \times (23 - 5) \\\\
&= 10 \times 18 \\\\
&= 180
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$180$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= (23 - 13) \times (23 - 5) \\\\
&= (23 - 13) \times (23 - 5) \\\\
&= 10 \times 18 \\\\
&= 180
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$180$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Richard obtains a final answer of $0$. What number might he have thought of? Give all possibilities.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$(a - 13) \times (a - 5)  = 0$

$a = 13$

or 

$a = 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13$ or $5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$(a - 13) \times (a - 5)  = 0$
<emptyline>
$a = 13$
<emptyline>
or 
<emptyline>
$a = 5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$13$ or $5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='review'>

<div class='question question'>

Snow White and the seven dwarves work in a mining company. 
As Chief Executive, Snow White earns as much as all the dwarves put together. 
Sneezy gets $24$ gold galleons a day. Sleepy earns $50\%$ more than Happy. 
Bashful earns twice as much as Grumpy who earns one more galleon than Sneezy. 
Doc gets $24$ galleons a day more than Dopey and between them they get four times that of Sneezy. 
Sleepy earns seven-eighths of Sneezy's salary.

Complete the table below to work out how much Snow White earns in a day.

Bashful Doc Dopey Grumpy Happy Sleepy Sneezy Snow White Gold Galleons Snow White

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-CEFEC184-01C9-49CB-9761-F2A3E70E6B6F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Write down two numbers that add to $14$ and multiply to $48$. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $=a$

Let the second number $=b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b               &= 14 \\\\
a \times b          &= 48
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $48$.
- The addition of the $2$ numbers is $14$.

Factors of $48 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24 \ \ \text{and} \ \ 48$

Factor pairs of $48$ that add upto $14 = 6,8$

| Factors   |  Product  |    Addittion      |
|:-------:  |:--------: |:----------------: |
| $6, 8$    |   $48$    |       $14$        |

A quick check shows $6$ and $8$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers are $= 6, 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$ and $8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $=a$
<emptyline>
Let the second number $=b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b               &= 14 \\\\
a \times b          &= 48
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.
<emptyline>
- The product of the $2$ numbers is $48$.
- The addition of the $2$ numbers is $14$.
<emptyline>
Factors of $48 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24 \ \ \text{and} \ \ 48$
<emptyline>
Factor pairs of $48$ that add upto $14 = 6,8$
<emptyline>
| Factors   |  Product  |    Addittion      |
|:-------:  |:--------: |:----------------: |
| $6, 8$    |   $48$    |       $14$        |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows $6$ and $8$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. rtq_abbr_md_quadratic_note*
<emptyline>
The two numbers are $= 6, 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-CE041EF6-D2B5-42A8-8F9B-E36FA1D03E08'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Sam thinks of a number.

He multiplies that number by $5$. 

Then he subtracts $12$. 

Then he divides by $3$. 

Finally he adds $17$.

His answer is $38$.

What number did Sam originally think of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number that Sam thought of $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {(5a -12)}{3} + 17                                    &= 38 \\\\
\dfrac {5a -12}{3}                                           &= 38 - 17\\\\
\dfrac {5a -12}{3}                                           &= 21 \\\\
5a - 12                                                      &= 21 \times 3 \\\\
5a - 12                                                      &= 63 \\\\  
5a                                                           &= 63 + 12 \\\\ 
5a                                                           &= 75 \\\\
a                                                            &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

The number that Sam thought of $= 15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number that Sam thought of $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {(5a -12)}{3} + 17                                    &= 38 \\\\
\dfrac {5a -12}{3}                                           &= 38 - 17\\\\
\dfrac {5a -12}{3}                                           &= 21 \\\\
5a - 12                                                      &= 21 \times 3 \\\\
5a - 12                                                      &= 63 \\\\  
5a                                                           &= 63 + 12 \\\\ 
5a                                                           &= 75 \\\\
a                                                            &= 15
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number that Sam thought of $= 15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-0052563B-523A-443E-B7A2-21267B36F737'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Elizabeth writes down:

One multiple of $13$ and two different factors of $77$.

Elizabeth adds up her three numbers. Her answer is greater than fifty but less than sixty.

What three numbers could Elizabeth have written down?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the two factors of 77 $= a, b$

Let the multiple of 13 $= c$

As per the question,

50 < a + b + c < 60

Factors of $77 = 1, 7, 11, 77$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Lets find out the value of a, b and c which satisfies the condition above.

We can ignore 77 as it is greater than 60.

|  c   |    a,b    |          sum           |  50 < sum < 60  |
|:----:|:---------:|:----------------------:|:---------------:|
| $13$ |  $1,7$    |  $13 + 1 + 7 = 21$     |       No        |
| $13$ |  $1,11$   |  $13 + 1 + 11 = 25$    |       No        |
| $13$ |  $7,11$   |  $13 + 7 + 11 = 31$    |       No        |
| $26$ |  $1,7$    |  $26 + 1 + 7 = 34$     |       No        |
| $26$ |  $1,11$   |  $26 + 1 + 11 = 38$    |       No        |
| $26$ |  $7,11$   |  $26 + 7 + 11 = 44$    |       No        |
| $39$ |  $1,7$    |  $39 + 1 + 7 = 47$     |       No        |
| $39$ |  $1,11$   |  $39 + 1 + 11 = 51$    |       Yes       |
| $39$ |  $7,11$   |  $39 + 7 + 11 = 57$    |       Yes       |

A quick check shows that $a = 1,b = 11,c = 39$ or $a = 7,b = 11,c = 39$ meets all the criteria above.

Numbers Elizabeth could have written down $= 1,11,39$ or $7,11,39$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1,11,39$ or $7,11,39$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Rinesh started painting his house two years ago. During that year, Rinesh painted one-third of his house.

Last year, Rinesh painted another five-twelfths of his house.

What fraction of his house does Rinesh need to paint this year, in order to finish completely painting his house?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction left to paint}       &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{3} - \dfrac{5}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{1 \times 12}{1 \times 12} - \dfrac{1 \times 4}{3 \times 4} - \dfrac{5}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{12}{12} - \dfrac{4}{12} - \dfrac{5}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{12 - 4 - 5}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{12 - 9}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{3}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{3}{4 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{\cancel{3}}{4 \times \cancel{3}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{1}{4}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{1}{4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction left to paint}       &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{3} - \dfrac{5}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{1 \times 12}{1 \times 12} - \dfrac{1 \times 4}{3 \times 4} - \dfrac{5}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{12}{12} - \dfrac{4}{12} - \dfrac{5}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{12 - 4 - 5}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{12 - 9}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{3}{12} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{3}{4 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{\cancel{3}}{4 \times \cancel{3}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{1}{4}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{1}{4}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Bijal started weeding her garden two weeks ago. During that week, she weeded one-fifth of her garden.

Last week, Bijal weeded two and a half times as much of her garden as she weeded two weeks ago.

What fraction of her garden does Bijal need to weed this week, in order to finish completely weeding her garden?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction left to weed}        &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{5} - \dfrac{1}{5} \times 2.5 \\\\
                                    &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{5} - \dfrac{2.5}{5} \\\\
                                    &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{5} - \dfrac{2.5}{2 \times 2.5} \\\\
                                    &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{5} - \dfrac{\cancel{2.5}}{2 \times \cancel{2.5}} \\\\
                                    &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{5} - \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{1 \times 10}{1 \times 10} - \dfrac{1 \times 2}{5 \times 2} - \dfrac{1 \times 5}{2 \times 5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{10}{10} - \dfrac{2}{10} - \dfrac{5}{10} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{10 - 2 - 5}{10} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{10 - 7}{10} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{3}{10}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{3}{10}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction left to weed}        &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{5} - \dfrac{1}{5} \times 2.5 \\\\
                                    &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{5} - \dfrac{2.5}{5} \\\\
                                    &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{5} - \dfrac{2.5}{2 \times 2.5} \\\\
                                    &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{5} - \dfrac{\cancel{2.5}}{2 \times \cancel{2.5}} \\\\
                                    &= 1 - \dfrac{1}{5} - \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{1 \times 10}{1 \times 10} - \dfrac{1 \times 2}{5 \times 2} - \dfrac{1 \times 5}{2 \times 5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{10}{10} - \dfrac{2}{10} - \dfrac{5}{10} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{10 - 2 - 5}{10} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{10 - 7}{10} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{3}{10}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{3}{10}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Nina is painting garden gnomes. She has four-fifths of a litre of paint. Each garden gnome needs one-twentieth of a litre of paint. How many garden gnomes can she paint?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of gardens can she paint}      &= \dfrac{4}{5} \times 12 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac{48}{5} \\\\
                                            &= 48 \div 5 \\\\
                                            &= 9 \text{ remainder } 3 \\\\
                                            &\approx 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of gardens can she paint}      &= \dfrac{4}{5} \times 12 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac{48}{5} \\\\
                                            &= 48 \div 5 \\\\
                                            &= 9 \text{ remainder } 3 \\\\
                                            &\approx 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$8 + 4  \times \boxed{\phantom{1}} = 36$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
8 + 4a   &= 36 \\\\
4a       &= 36 - 8 \\\\
4a       &= 28 \\\\
a        &= \dfrac {28} {4} \\\\
a        &= \dfrac {4 \times 7} {4} \\\\
a        &= \dfrac {\cancel{4} \times 7} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
a        &=  7
\end{aligned}
$

Missing number $=7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $= a$
<emptyline>

a        &= \dfrac {4 \times 7} {4} \\\\
a        &= \dfrac {\cancel{4} \times 7} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
a        &=  7
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Missing number $=7$
</code></pre>

\end{aligned}
$
